**Fail-closed evidence rule:** Include explicit baseline artifact tasks, final-QA artifact tasks, and coverage-comparison tasks for each in-scope language when policy requires coverage. If any required baseline artifact, QA artifact, or coverage-comparison artifact is missing, the audit verdict must be BLOCKED or INCOMPLETE, never PASS.

**Evidence accounting rule:** Record the expected artifact path or location in each evidence-producing task. Do not mark evidence-backed work complete without the artifact.

> Formatting contract (do not "fix"): a downstream tool harvests backtick-delimited, whitespace-free path tokens from this file as the change footprint. Backticked single-token paths therefore appear only in the `## Write Set` appendix and for evidence paths under this feature folder. Every other repository path, including every path this change deliberately does not touch, is written as plain prose or inside a multi-word command span. Identifier hygiene: the developer account name, the host name, the eight-dot-three short form of the account and every absolute host path are referred to by category or by the placeholders `<repo-root>`, `<user-profile>`, `<user>` and `<host>`. No drive-rooted profile path is written contiguously anywhere in this file, so this file passes the guard it plans. Every command payload that names a repository path this plan does not create, modify or delete (a script it invokes, a solution, checkpoint or configuration file it reads or tests, a folder it scans, enumerates or names in a porcelain scope, a content literal it matches, or an edit line that quotes such a folder) is carried in a fenced text block and never in an inline code span, for the same harvesting reason: the extractor reads inline spans only, so a fenced payload contributes no path token. Three operand classes are left inline because none is an accepted token shape and none names a specific unwritten file or folder: the colon-bang pathspec exclusion (the form that keeps the governance directory out of a scan), which the extractor rejects because its leading segment carries a colon; a separator-free scope root (the repository root dot, scripts, tests, .github); and a quoted extension glob used as a pathspec or porcelain scope (for example the csproj, cs, xml, config and trx globs, and the test-project and docs/features globs), whose leading quote the extractor does not accept. This file uses LF line endings.

## Environment and conventions (binding on every task)

- **C1 Working directory.** Every command runs with the current directory at the worktree root of the branch above. Every path in a command is repository-relative. The executor's worktree may be a different checkout from the one this plan was authored in; P0-T2 verifies identity. Revision 1.15 (F8, round 12; corrected round 13): in any command whose text contains a removal verb anywhere, whether as a call or inside a search pattern (the P4-T7 payload's RemoveAt call, the first MSTEST-COVERAGE payload that P6-T9 runs, the P6-T12 FILE-IO pattern and the P6-T38 download step), the item worktree path that prefixes the payload is assembled at run time with the worktrees segment itself split across two string literals, for example the user profile folder joined with "/repos/TaskMaster/.claude/" + "work" + "trees/" + the worktree leaf. The worktree-removal hook refuses a command that carries the governance directory name, a slash and the word worktrees as one contiguous segment together with a removal verb. A join that keeps that segment inside one literal, such as drive root, repository folder and worktree leaf joined with Join-Path, was refused in preflight round 12.
- **C2 Command channel and quoting.** Non-git commands are written as `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '<payload>'` with the payload in single quotes and every inner string literal in double quotes, so neither a Bash nor a PowerShell parent expands `$` before pwsh runs. Re-quoting must preserve that property. Git commands are written bare. A payload that must expose an exit code ends with an explicit `exit`.
- **C3 Evidence schema.** Every artifact named in this plan is Markdown under docs/features/active/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927/evidence/<kind>/ and carries `Timestamp:` (yyyy-MM-ddTHH-mm), `Command:`, `EXIT_CODE:` and `Output Summary:`. A task that runs several commands names exactly one invocation's exit code as the `EXIT_CODE:` row and records the others as labelled lines in `Output Summary:`. `ExpectedExitCode:` is declared only where the task text says so, and always carries the value the task expects that run to produce.
- **C4 Identifier rule.** No artifact, log excerpt, commit message or plan edit may contain the account name, the host name, the eight-dot-three form, or an absolute host path. Every identifier-bearing command derives its values at run time from `$env:USERNAME`, `$env:COMPUTERNAME` and `$env:USERPROFILE` (the eight-dot-three leaf from the file-system object). Artifacts record counts, leaf names of tools, and the placeholders `<repo-root>`, `<user-profile>`, `<user>` and `<host>` only. A tool path resolved through vswhere is recorded by its leaf name only. The value of `$env:TEMP` is never written; only the expression.
- **C5 Scratch area.** SCRATCH denotes the directory the expression `Join-Path $env:TEMP "hygiene-927"` names. The redaction helper, its log, the removal list and every raw tool output that is not under the ignored coverage directory live there. Nothing under SCRATCH is ever copied into the repository. The redaction helper is referred to by role ("the redaction helper"); its file name is Invoke-IdentifierRedaction.ps1 inside SCRATCH.
- **C6 Ignored working-tree output.** Raw tool output written inside the repository goes only under coverage/logs/ and coverage/test-results/ (both ignored by the coverage/* rule at .gitignore line 150; it sat at line 144 when this plan was authored and moved when P2-T2 inserted six lines above it, re-derived in round 12 per F7). P0-T2 creates coverage/logs/.
- **C7 BASE-SHA and MERGE-BASE.** P0-T2 records the 40-character `git rev-parse HEAD` as `BASE-SHA:` and `git merge-base origin/main HEAD` as `MERGE-BASE:`. Revision 1.15 (F4, round 12): every diff gate that runs in Phase 6 (P6-T5, P6-T11, P6-T13, P6-T14 and P6-T24) is anchored at the three-dot range origin/main...HEAD, written inside its command span, so that it measures only this branch's own changes since its merge base with main; the branch merges origin/main before any Phase 6 diff check runs, so a two-point diff from BASE-SHA to HEAD would count every path main gained through that merge (preflight round 11 measured 215 such paths against the Write Set). The four payloads of P6-T5, P6-T11, P6-T13 and P6-T14 also print `MERGE-BASE-NOW=` from `git merge-base origin/main HEAD` (P6-T24's check-off diff is a bare git command and prints none), recorded and never asserted equal to the P0-T2 `MERGE-BASE:` value, which the merge necessarily moves (F2, round 12). BASE-SHA is kept only as the P0-T11 formatting reference that P6-T5 compares against and as the operand of the second AC19 ancestry check in P6-T14; where the bare token BASE-SHA appears, the executor transcribes the literal recorded value. No task asserts an expected HEAD.
- **C8 Porcelain gates assert scope, never emptiness.** Every `git status --porcelain` clause names the path classes that must be absent (source, project, workflow, or script trees as the task states); paths under this feature folder and under .claude/agent-memory/ are admitted without enumeration, and the presence of the plan file is never asserted either way. `--untracked-files=all` is used only where a positive per-file enumeration is required.
- **C9 Commit form.** Commits use `git add -- <pathspecs>` followed by `git commit -m "<subject>" -m "Co-Authored-By: MODEL-NAME noreply@anthropic.com"`. The bare token MODEL-NAME in every commit span of this plan stands for the executing session's own model name; the executor transcribes it in the same bracket-free form (the model name, one space, the noreply address), so the trailer names whichever model executes the plan and this plan pins no model literal. No angle bracket, dollar sign or backtick appears on a commit line (the trailer is deliberately written without angle brackets so the pre-implementation gate can parse the line). When the executor's own session instructs an additional Claude-Session trailer, it is appended as one more `-m` operand in the same bracket-free form; the session URL is never pinned by this plan. Commits that stage only paths under this feature folder are written with a trailing `-- docs/features/active/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927` pathspec so they pass the pre-implementation gate's bookkeeping exemption. Commits that stage scripts, tests, workflows, the ignore file or C# files require the orchestrator-seeded checkpoint P0-T3 verifies. The executor never runs any `git update-index` command and never pushes.
- **C10 Pester exit convention.** A direct `Invoke-Pester -Configuration` run exits 0 whatever the result, so every Pester payload emits its counts on one line (`PESTER Passed=.. Failed=.. Skipped=.. Total=..`) and then ends `if ($r.FailedCount -gt 0 -or $r.PassedCount -eq 0) { exit 1 } else { exit 0 }`. The counts line, not the exit code, is the primary observation.
- **C11 PoshQC MCP pairing.** Where the PowerShell rule names an MCP command, the task runs it unconditionally for the policy record and pairs it unconditionally with the direct command that supplies the figures. If the MCP tool is not callable in the executor's session, the task records `POSHQC MCP UNAVAILABLE` on the channel line and continues on the direct command; this is a recorded observation, never a halt. Revision 1.14 (Ruling 1, run coordinator): for the final PowerShell gates P6-T2 and P6-T3 the PoshQC MCP tool is the sole local source and no direct analyzer or Invoke-Pester command is paired with it; the analyze tool's `ok` flag is the lint result (a pass means zero findings and the tool reports no count), the test tool's result is read from the JUnit document it writes, and the coverage figures come from the CI Pester job in P6-T38. In those two tasks a `POSHQC MCP UNAVAILABLE` channel line is recorded, the step is recorded as `NOT OBSERVED`, no substitute command is run, the loop is not restarted for it, and the dependent check-offs (P6-T19, P6-T22, P6-T30, P6-T35) record `NOT MET` naming that line.
- **C12 Stop branches.** A task that names a `STOP:` condition stops the run at that task and reports; it does not choose a substitute action. The complete stop list: P0-T2 `STOP: WRONG BRANCH`, `STOP: DIRTY SOURCE TREE` and `STOP: PRE-EXISTING STAGED PATHS`; P0-T3 `STOP: PRE-IMPLEMENTATION GATE NOT SEEDED` and `STOP: CHECKPOINT NOT READY`; P0-T4 `STOP: COMMAND CHANNEL REFUSED`; P0-T7 `STOP: RESTORE REWROTE PROJECT FILE`; P0-T17 `STOP: IDENTIFIER IN BUILD INPUT`; P1-T12 `STOP: AC1 ARITHMETIC MISMATCH`; P4-T2 `STOP: HOST TOKEN AMBIGUOUS` and `STOP: IDENTIFIER OUTSIDE RULE BOUNDS`; P4-T4 `STOP: REDACTION OUTSIDE WRITE SET`; P6-T5 `STOP: FORMAT OUTSIDE WRITE SET`; P6-T14 `STOP: SCOPE VIOLATION`; P6-T38 `STOP: CI FIGURE NOT REPORTED` (Ruling 1: when the CI run does not produce a figure the plan needs, the task stops and reports). No other task stops; every other observed deviation is recorded and the run continues.
- **C13 Dangling Markdown links.** Historical evidence Markdown that links to a removed raw document is tolerated (spec, Scope & Non-Goals). A reviewer must not read a dangling link as an incomplete task; no task rewrites prose links.
- **C14 Feature folder.** FEATURE denotes docs/features/active/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927 in prose. Evidence paths in task lines are written in full.

## Decisions (D1 to D18)

- **D1 One profile-path finding per file.** `Find-UserProfilePathMatch` returns a record for every matching line, but `Invoke-RepositoryHygieneMain` emits one `HYGIENE profile-path <path>:<line>` line per file, carrying the first matching line number. Reason: AC1 defines the pre-sweep findings total as the raw-document population plus the profile-path FILE population, which is a per-file count; a per-line count could not satisfy AC1 by construction. The same file may additionally produce one `HYGIENE raw-document <path>` line, so a raw document that also carries a path contributes two findings, exactly as the two gate populations overlap.
- **D2 Binary records.** A record whose eol listing reads i/-text is read for content only when its first two bytes are FF FE or FE FF (a UTF-16 byte-order mark); otherwise it is classified by extension only (rule A) and skipped for rule B. Reason: UTF-16 files are the one class of tracked text that `git grep -I` omits (it treats them as binary), so gate 4 alone undercounts by exactly the UTF-16 files that carry a profile path. P0-T17 counts those files separately (`UTF16-FILES=`, `UTF16-PROFILE-FILES=`, `UTF16-IDENTIFIER-FILES=`), and the AC1 operand `PROFILE-PATH-FILES:` is GATE4 plus `UTF16-PROFILE-FILES=`, which is the population the guard scans (one such file is expected: the msbuild analyzer baseline in the issue 155 archive folder, whose folder glob is in the Write Set).
- **D3 Unreadable rule.** The rule name `unreadable` is emitted when the ReadContent delegate throws. The default delegate decodes by byte-order mark (UTF-8, UTF-16 little-endian, UTF-16 big-endian) and otherwise as UTF-8 without throwing on invalid bytes, so the default never produces this finding on a readable file; a seventh orchestration test pins the branch with a throwing delegate. Reason: a throwing UTF-8 decoder would turn any Latin-1 tracked file into a permanent finding the sweep cannot fix.
- **D4 No list switch.** The entry point gains no `-ListRawDocuments` switch. The removal list is derived from the guard's own output: the paths on lines beginning `HYGIENE raw-document ` from the P1-T12 pre-sweep run, saved under SCRATCH. Reason: the default behaviour stays the only behaviour, and the classifier that CI enforces is the classifier that drove the removal.
- **D5 MSBuild resolution.** `msbuild` is not on PATH in this environment. Every msbuild task resolves the executable in the same payload through vswhere under the thirty-two-bit Program Files Visual Studio Installer directory with `-latest -prerelease -products * -requires Microsoft.Component.MSBuild -find "MSBuild/**/Bin/MSBuild.exe"`, records the leaf name only, and passes the argument list character-for-character as CLAUDE.md writes it. The repository build wrapper is not used because it runs the package-reference synchroniser that rewrites project files. Console output is captured with `Tee-Object` under coverage/logs/ at the default normal verbosity, where each project's csc command line (carrying the /out:obj\Debug\ token) is echoed and a skipped CoreCompile target would print a `Skipping target "CoreCompile"` line.
- **D6 Redaction helper.** Authored by the executor in P4-T1 under SCRATCH, never committed, in PowerShell 7. Population: the records of `git ls-files -z --eol -- . ":!.claude/"`, skipping i/-text records without a UTF-16 byte-order mark. Rules 1 to 7 are the spec's Proposed Fix table and rule 8 is D17, all built at run time from the environment; the account alternation is the escaped `$env:USERNAME`, the escaped profile leaf when it differs, the escaped eight-dot-three leaf, and the three legacy user tokens named in research section 3.4. Rules 1 to 5 and rule 8 use RegexOptions.IgnoreCase; rules 6 and 7 are case-sensitive (D18). Rule 3 is skipped by path for .mcp.json and .codex/config.toml. Rules 6, 7 and 8 are skipped by path under this feature folder (spec Resolved tensions item 3 for rules 6 and 7; D17 for rule 8). Rules 6 and 7 consume an enclosing angle-bracket pair when one is present, so an already-bracketed legacy token becomes exactly one canonical placeholder. Rules 3 and 5 keep the spec's boundaries exactly (a hyphen-suffixed host form and an alphanumeric-embedded account form are not redacted); P4-T2 counts those two forms before the sweep and stops the run when either count is non-zero, so the residual gates cannot be reached with a form the rules do not cover. Bytes in, bytes out: byte-order mark detected and re-emitted (UTF-8 with mark, UTF-16 little-endian, UTF-16 big-endian: a UTF-16 file is decoded with the encoding its mark names and re-encoded with that same encoding and mark), same encoding, no newline normalisation, a file written only when its substitution count is non-zero. XML-family extensions (.xml, .trx, .coveragexml, .csproj, .props, .targets, .config, .resx, .runsettings, .xaml, .nuspec, .settings) receive the escaped placeholder forms and are re-parsed with an `[xml]` cast after writing; the expected count of such files is zero. A `-DryRun` switch counts matches without writing. The log at SCRATCH\redaction.log records one entry per written file (a file with a zero substitution count has no entry, so the log's entry set is the written set): path, per-rule substitution counts, byte-order-mark class before and after, and whether the path lies under this feature folder; the helper's console output includes `FEATURE-FOLDER-WRITTEN=` (the count of written paths under this feature folder, expected 0). The artifacts record counts only.
- **D7 Fixture rewrites.** The fourteen fixture files are rewritten by hand in Phase 3 per the spec's triage table and the literal table in `### Fixture literals`, never by the helper: their roots change and their user-segment tokens are retained.
- **D8 Ignore-pattern collision.** The new `*cobertura*.xml` pattern also matches the coverage runner's default projection file name under the ignored coverage directory. This item's evidence is Markdown, so nothing here is affected; a future item that copies that projection under an evidence tree must rename it without the marker. None of the eighteen retained projections carries the marker in its name (re-derived: all eighteen end in .jacoco.xml or start with coverage- or p0-/p2-/p5-/p9-).
- **D9 README consistency edits.** In addition to the three AC15 edits, the three sentences that say "six callee" or "six contexts" (lines 3, 17 and 174 of .github/workflows/README.md as re-derived) are changed to "seven", because leaving them would make the README self-contradictory. No other README text changes.
- **D10 AC13 local versus CI.** The local coverage route figures are compared against the P0-T14 baseline as AC13 words it (not below). The comparison additionally records lines-valid on both sides; when the two lines-valid figures differ by more than one percent of the baseline the artifact says the denominators are incomparable. When either run did not write its summary file (the second MSTEST-COVERAGE payload prints `SUMMARY: NOT WRITTEN`), the passed and failed counts are read from the TRX `Counters` element and every coverage figure of that run is recorded as `UNAVAILABLE`; a coverage figure recorded as `UNAVAILABLE` on either side makes the AC13 comparison NOT MET. AC13's check-off requires the not-below result; the CI mstest-coverage context is recorded in the AC16 task and is the authoritative pass named by the spec.
- **D11 PowerShell format scope.** The write-mode format runs over the folders scripts/hygiene and tests/scripts/hygiene through the MCP formatter and over the single modified helper test file through `Invoke-Formatter` on that file's content, never over scripts/vscode or the whole tests/scripts/vscode folder, because the formatter rewrites an untouched production script there. A restore branch checks out any tests/scripts/vscode path other than the helper test that the pass rewrote and records it.
- **D12 Format observation.** The formatter's `Formatted N files` line is a processed count, not a rewrite count. Rewrite is observed by SHA-256 of each in-scope file before and after the pass (`Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 -LiteralPath` reading the Hash property only) and, for the repository-wide csharpier pass, additionally by `git status --porcelain -- "*.cs" "*.xml" "*.config"` after the pass.
- **D13 Test-run counts.** On an all-green run vstest prints no `Failed:` line; the test-result summary the coverage route writes carries passed, failed and the derived skipped figure, and the plan reads the counts from that summary, not from the console; when the summary was not written, the counts are read from the TRX `Counters` element with the P3-T16 regular expression (D10).
- **D14 Scoped run classifier.** The Phase 3 scoped test run identifies the thirteen rewritten C# test classes by `FullyQualifiedName~<ClassSimpleName>` clauses joined with `|`, and proves each class ran by counting distinct `className` attribute values in the run's TRX under the ignored results directory. A run matching zero tests is a failure.
- **D15 Pester version.** Every Pester payload imports `Pester -RequiredVersion 5.6.1`, the version the Pester callee pins. P0-T9 installs it to CurrentUser scope only when absent.
- **D16 Orchestration checkpoint.** The executor never seeds or edits artifacts/orchestration/orchestrator-state.json. P0-T3 reads it and stops the run before any non-exempt commit when it is absent.
- **D17 Rule 8 (added by this plan).** A drive letter, a colon, a separator run, the profile parent (case-insensitive), a separator run and a user segment of one or more characters from the class letter, digit, underscore, dot, tilde and hyphen, is replaced by `<user-profile>`. It is applied after rule 7 with RegexOptions.IgnoreCase. Reason: the spec's in-scope list names absolute profile paths, and AC9 requires gate four to reach zero. Rules 1 to 7 cover only account-segment paths, and 19 tracked files carry fixture or ellipsis segments. The pattern, written here unbackticked and with the separator run unbounded so that every gate-four match is a rule-8 match: [a-z]:[\\/]+users[\\/]+[a-z0-9_.~-]+ under IgnoreCase. The replacement begins with a less-than sign, so a second run matches nothing (P4-T5). Ordering guarantee: P3-T18 commits the fourteen fixture rewrites before P4-T1 authors the helper, so when P4-T4 runs no fixture file matches the profile pattern (the Phase 3 probes assert `PATTERN=0` per file) and rule 8 cannot rewrite one; P4-T4 `CODE-PATHS=0` is the observation. Re-derived at planning time, the code-typed tracked files that match the pattern are exactly the fourteen Phase 3 fixtures, .claude/settings.json (outside the helper's population), raw XML documents the Phase 2 removal deletes, and two process-tree JSON evidence files under the issue 400 archive folder (a Write Set glob; JSON is outside the P4-T4 `CODE-PATHS=` extension set, and the account-segment rules already rewrite them). The helper's population excludes .claude/ (D6), and rule 8 is skipped by path under this feature folder, which carries zero gate-four matches (re-derived), so `FEATURE-FOLDER-WRITTEN=0` remains the expectation and a profile-pattern line written into this folder later is reported by gate four in P4-T8 rather than rewritten. The spec's rule table carries the same rule 8 with this reason.
- **D18 Legacy-token case.** Gate nine and rule 7 are case-sensitive as specified. The upper-case user-token forms are outside rule 7 and remain. They are not identifiers and do not trip the guard. P0-T17 and P4-T8 record the case-insensitive file count `LEGACY-USER-ANYCASE-FILES=` over the two legacy user tokens, recorded and not gated; P6-T26 records that residual next to `GATE9=0`. The spec's Resolved tensions item 7 states the same decision.

### Gate command reference

The identifier gates. Each is one pwsh segment; values are derived at run time and never typed. GATE3 guards against an empty short name so an empty fixed string can never match every file.

- GATE1: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '$a = $env:USERNAME; $l = Split-Path -Leaf $env:USERPROFILE; "GATE1=" + @(git grep -I -l -i -F -e $a -e $l -- . ":!.claude/" ":!.mcp.json" ":!.codex/config.toml").Count'`
- GATE2: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '$m = $env:COMPUTERNAME; "GATE2=" + @(git grep -I -l -i -F -e $m -- . ":!.claude/").Count'`
- GATE3: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '$s = (New-Object -ComObject Scripting.FileSystemObject).GetFolder($env:USERPROFILE).ShortName; if ([string]::IsNullOrEmpty($s)) { "GATE3=SHORTNAME-EMPTY" } else { "GATE3=" + @(git grep -I -l -i -F -e $s -- . ":!.claude/").Count }'`
- GATE4: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '"GATE4=" + @(git grep -I -l -i -E -e "[a-z]:[\\/]+users[\\/]+[a-z0-9_.~-]" -- . ":!.claude/").Count'`
- GATE5A: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '"GATE5A=" + @(git ls-files -- "*.trx" "*cobertura*.xml" "*.coverage" "*.coveragexml").Count'`
- GATE5B: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '"GATE5B=" + @(git grep -l -E "^<(coverage|results|TestRun|CoverageSession)" -- "*.xml" "*.trx").Count'`
- GATE5C: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '"GATE5C=" + @(git grep -l -E "<(class|sourcefile|method|line)[ >]" -- "docs/features/*.xml").Count'`
- GATE6: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '& ./scripts/hygiene/Test-RepositoryHygiene.ps1 2>&1 | Tee-Object -FilePath coverage/logs/927-guard-run.log; exit $LASTEXITCODE'`
- GATE8: two bare git invocations, fenced below in their order (the two probe paths name no tracked file, but the second carries a recognized extension and the extractor would record it as a concrete path claim), each followed by reading its exit code in the same channel and recorded as `GATE8-TRX-EXIT` and `GATE8-COBERTURA-EXIT` respectively. A single invocation over both paths exits 0 when either path is ignored, so it cannot prove both patterns; the two invocations are required.

```text
git check-ignore -q docs/features/x/a.trx
git check-ignore -q docs/features/x/b.cobertura.xml
```

- GATE9: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '"GATE9=" + @(git grep -I -l -e redacted-account -e redacted-user -e REDACTED-HOST -- . ":!.claude/" ":!docs/features/active/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927/").Count'`
- GATE10: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '"GATE10=" + @(git ls-files --eol -- . ":!.claude/" | Select-String -SimpleMatch "i/-text").Count'`
- PESTER-HYGIENE: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command 'Import-Module Pester -RequiredVersion 5.6.1; $c = New-PesterConfiguration; $c.Run.Path = "tests/scripts/hygiene"; $c.Run.PassThru = $true; $c.Output.Verbosity = "Detailed"; $r = Invoke-Pester -Configuration $c; "PESTER Passed=$($r.PassedCount) Failed=$($r.FailedCount) Skipped=$($r.SkippedCount) Total=$($r.TotalCount)"; foreach ($t in $r.Tests) { "IT| " + $t.ExpandedPath + " | " + $t.Result }; if ($r.FailedCount -gt 0 -or $r.PassedCount -eq 0) { exit 1 } else { exit 0 }'`
- PESTER-CI-SHAPE (the Pester callee's configuration with the hygiene folders added): the payload fenced below; it names the callee's two existing script folders and their test folders, which this plan does not write. The baseline form in P0-T15 omits the two hygiene entries. Revision 1.14: this payload is consumed only by the completed tasks P0-T15 and P1-T9; P6-T3 no longer runs it (Ruling 1), and the final coverage figures are read from the CI Pester job's log and its pester-coverage artifact in P6-T38.

```text
pwsh -NoProfile -Command 'Import-Module Pester -RequiredVersion 5.6.1; $c = New-PesterConfiguration; $c.Run.Path = @("tests/scripts/dependencies", "tests/scripts/hygiene", "tests/scripts/vscode"); $c.Run.PassThru = $true; $c.Output.Verbosity = "Detailed"; $c.CodeCoverage.Enabled = $true; $c.CodeCoverage.Path = @("scripts/dependencies", "scripts/hygiene", "scripts/vscode"); $c.CodeCoverage.OutputFormat = "JaCoCo"; $c.CodeCoverage.OutputPath = "coverage/pester-coverage.xml"; $r = Invoke-Pester -Configuration $c; "PESTER Passed=$($r.PassedCount) Failed=$($r.FailedCount) Skipped=$($r.SkippedCount) Total=$($r.TotalCount)"; [xml]$j = Get-Content "coverage/pester-coverage.xml" -Raw; $lc = @($j.SelectNodes("/report/counter")) | Where-Object { $_.type -eq "LINE" }; $cov = [int]$lc.covered; $tot = $cov + [int]$lc.missed; "COVERAGE LinePercent=" + (100 * $cov / $tot).ToString("0.00") + " Covered=$cov Total=$tot"; foreach ($p in @($j.SelectNodes("/report/package"))) { $pc = @($p.SelectNodes("counter")) | Where-Object { $_.type -eq "LINE" }; "PACKAGE| " + $p.name + " | covered=" + $pc.covered + " missed=" + $pc.missed; foreach ($sf in @($p.SelectNodes("sourcefile"))) { $fc = @($sf.SelectNodes("counter")) | Where-Object { $_.type -eq "LINE" }; "FILE| " + $p.name + "/" + $sf.name + " | covered=" + $fc.covered + " missed=" + $fc.missed } }; if ($r.FailedCount -gt 0 -or $r.PassedCount -eq 0) { exit 1 } else { exit 0 }'
```

- MSBUILD-ANALYZERS: the payload fenced below; it names the solution file, which this plan does not write.

```text
pwsh -NoProfile -Command '$vw = Join-Path ${env:ProgramFiles(x86)} "Microsoft Visual Studio\Installer\vswhere.exe"; $mb = & $vw -latest -prerelease -products * -requires Microsoft.Component.MSBuild -find "MSBuild/**/Bin/MSBuild.exe" | Select-Object -First 1; "MSBUILD-LEAF=" + (Split-Path -Leaf $mb); & $mb TaskMaster.sln /t:Rebuild /m /p:Configuration=Debug "/p:Platform=Any CPU" /p:EnableNETAnalyzers=true /p:EnforceCodeStyleInBuild=true 2>&1 | Tee-Object -FilePath coverage/logs/927-analyzers.log; exit $LASTEXITCODE'
```

- MSBUILD-NULLABLE: the same payload with the argument list fenced below and the log coverage/logs/927-nullable.log.

```text
TaskMaster.sln /t:Rebuild /m /p:Configuration=Debug "/p:Platform=Any CPU" /p:TreatWarningsAsErrors=true
```

- MSBUILD-OBSERVE: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '$l = Get-Content -LiteralPath "coverage/logs/927-analyzers.log"; "OUT-LINES=" + @($l | Select-String -SimpleMatch "/out:obj\Debug\").Count; "SKIP-CORECOMPILE=" + @($l | Select-String -SimpleMatch "Skipping target ""CoreCompile""").Count; "ZERO-ERRORS=" + @($l | Where-Object { $_ -match "^\s*0 Error\(s\)\s*$" }).Count; "SUCCEEDED=" + @($l | Where-Object { $_ -match "^Build succeeded\." }).Count'` (substitute the nullable log name for the nullable pass). `ZERO-ERRORS` is a whole-line match because `0 Error(s)` is a substring of `10 Error(s)`.
- MSTEST-COVERAGE: two payloads. The first is fenced below (it invokes the coverage runner under scripts/vscode, which this plan does not write) and is followed by the second, which names only ignored outputs under the coverage directory and stays inline: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command '$s = "coverage/test-results/mstest-coverage-run.summary.txt"; if (Test-Path $s) { Get-Content -LiteralPath $s } else { "SUMMARY: NOT WRITTEN" }; $t = "coverage/test-results/mstest-coverage-run.trx"; if (Test-Path $t) { $x = Get-Content -LiteralPath $t -Raw; $m = [regex]::Match($x, "<Counters total=""(\d+)"" executed=""(\d+)"" passed=""(\d+)"" failed=""(\d+)"""); "COUNTERS total=" + $m.Groups[1].Value + " executed=" + $m.Groups[2].Value + " passed=" + $m.Groups[3].Value + " failed=" + $m.Groups[4].Value } else { "TRX: NOT WRITTEN" }; Select-String -LiteralPath "coverage/logs/927-mstest.log" -Pattern "^First-party coverage: " | ForEach-Object { $_.Line }; $j = "coverage/coverage.cobertura.jacoco.xml"; if (Test-Path $j) { Get-Content -LiteralPath $j } else { "PROJECTION: NOT WRITTEN" }'`. The first payload begins by deleting the previous run's three outputs (ignored runtime files under the coverage directory, never staged) so that a failing or terminating run can never be read through the earlier run's summary or projection; the second payload prints `SUMMARY: NOT WRITTEN` when the summary is absent and reads the passed and failed counts from the TRX `Counters` element with the P3-T16 regular expression (D10). The route writes coverage/test-results/mstest-coverage-run.trx and its .summary.txt, coverage/coverage.cobertura.xml (discarded by the route when the summary was written) and coverage/coverage.cobertura.jacoco.xml, all under the ignored coverage directory.

```text
pwsh -NoProfile -Command 'Remove-Item -LiteralPath "coverage/test-results/mstest-coverage-run.summary.txt", "coverage/test-results/mstest-coverage-run.trx", "coverage/coverage.cobertura.jacoco.xml" -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue; try { & ./scripts/vscode/Invoke-MSTestWithCoverage.ps1 -SearchRoot . -Configuration Debug 2>&1 | Tee-Object -FilePath coverage/logs/927-mstest.log; exit 0 } catch { "TERMINATING: " + $_.Exception.Message; exit 1 }'
```

- CSHARPIER-FORMAT: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command 'dotnet tool run csharpier format . 2>&1 | Tee-Object -FilePath coverage/logs/927-csharpier-format.log; exit $LASTEXITCODE'`; CSHARPIER-CHECK: `pwsh -NoProfile -Command 'dotnet tool run csharpier check . 2>&1 | Tee-Object -FilePath coverage/logs/927-csharpier-check.log; exit $LASTEXITCODE'`. The success-case lines are `Formatted N files in Xms.` and `Checked N files in Xms.` respectively, where N is the processed count.

## Phases

### Phase 0 — Policy reads, environment bootstrap and baselines

- [x] [P0-T2] Record the tree-state baseline (branch, BASE-SHA, MERGE-BASE, both porcelain forms, worktree identity) in docs/features/active/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927/evidence/baseline/p0-t2-tree-state.md
  - Commands: `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD`; `git rev-parse HEAD`; `git merge-base origin/main HEAD`; `git status --porcelain`; `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all`; `git diff --cached --name-status`; and the pwsh payload fenced below, which tests for the solution file, a path this plan does not write.

```text
pwsh -NoProfile -Command 'New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path "coverage/logs" | Out-Null; "SLN=" + (Test-Path "TaskMaster.sln"); "FEATURE=" + (Test-Path "docs/features/active/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927/spec.md"); "PROMOTED=" + (Test-Path "docs/features/potential/promoted/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep.md"); "TRACKED-TOTAL=" + @(git ls-files).Count'
```

  - Acceptance: the branch line reads bug/evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927; `BASE-SHA:` and `MERGE-BASE:` each carry a 40-character value; `SLN=True`, `FEATURE=True` and `PROMOTED=True` (the promoted record and this feature folder are committed by the orchestrator's preparation commit before this task runs, so both are already in HEAD at BASE-SHA and no later task stages the promoted record); the collapsed porcelain output is recorded verbatim under `PreExistingWorktreePaths:` and contains no path ending in .cs, .csproj, .sln, .ps1, .yml or .gitignore and no path under scripts/, tests/ or .github/ (paths under this feature folder and under .claude/agent-memory/ are admitted); the `git diff --cached --name-status` output is recorded verbatim under `PreExistingStagedPaths:` and is empty; `TRACKED-TOTAL:` records the count (the research expects about 16,585; recorded, not gated). `STOP: WRONG BRANCH` when the branch line differs; `STOP: DIRTY SOURCE TREE` when a forbidden path class is present; `STOP: PRE-EXISTING STAGED PATHS` when anything is staged.
- [x] [P0-T3] Read the orchestration checkpoint at artifacts/orchestration/orchestrator-state.json read-only and record its readiness in docs/features/active/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927/evidence/baseline/p0-t3-checkpoint-read.md
  - Command: the pwsh payload fenced below, which reads the orchestration checkpoint under artifacts/orchestration, a file this plan never writes.

```text
pwsh -NoProfile -Command '$p = "artifacts/orchestration/orchestrator-state.json"; if (-not (Test-Path $p)) { "CHECKPOINT=ABSENT"; exit 0 }; $j = Get-Content -LiteralPath $p -Raw | ConvertFrom-Json; "CHECKPOINT=PRESENT"; "KEYS=" + (($j.PSObject.Properties.Name | Sort-Object) -join ","); "LIFECYCLE-READY=" + [bool]$j.lifecycle_ready; $in = ([string]$j."issue-num").Trim(); "ISSUE-NUM=" + [bool]$in; $ff = ([string]$j."feature-folder").Trim(); "FEATURE-ACTIVE=" + $ff.StartsWith("docs/features/active/"); "ROUTE=" + [bool]($j.route_id -or $j.path_selected)'
```

  - Reading rule: the two hyphenated keys are read top-level, trimmed and with no fallback, exactly as the pre-implementation gate's `Get-StringProperty` reads them at lines 63 to 75 of .claude/hooks/enforce-orchestration-preimplementation-gate.ps1 for its `Test-OrchestrationReady` check at lines 227 to 255, and `lifecycle_ready` is cast with `[bool]` exactly as line 243 of that check casts it, so an absent or null key prints `False` rather than an empty value; values are never printed.
  - Acceptance: the artifact records `CHECKPOINT=PRESENT`, the key names (values never recorded), `LIFECYCLE-READY=True`, `ISSUE-NUM=True`, `FEATURE-ACTIVE=True` and `ROUTE=True`. `CHECKPOINT=ABSENT` is `STOP: PRE-IMPLEMENTATION GATE NOT SEEDED`; any of the four flags printing other than `True` is `STOP: CHECKPOINT NOT READY`; both stop before P1-T1, because P1-T13, the Phase 6 loop commits and every later non-exempt commit would be denied. The executor never writes this file.

Both MCP branches complete this task. When the pwsh probe itself is refused by the executor's command channel (the invocation is denied rather than run, so no `PROBE-OK` line exists), the artifact records `COMMAND-CHANNEL: REFUSED` with the refusal text and the task ends in `STOP: COMMAND CHANNEL REFUSED`, because every later measurement in this plan is a pwsh payload.

### Phase 1 — Guard tests, guard code and the pre-sweep regression observation

Guard design contract (binding on P1-T1 to P1-T7; the function names and It names are the ones spec.md fixes):

- `Invoke-RepositoryHygieneMain -ReadContent <scriptblock>` (entry point file) returns one object with `Lines` (string array), `FindingCount` (int) and `ExitCode` (1 when FindingCount is non-zero, else 0) and writes nothing to any stream. It enumerates records through `Get-TrackedFileRecord`, drops records whose Path starts with the governance prefix .claude/ (the one prefix constant in the script), classifies each record with `Get-RawEvidenceDocumentKind` (reading content only for the xml extension), reads text through `Read-TrackedFileText` for rule B, emits `HYGIENE raw-document <path>` for a kind other than `none` and `jacoco-projection`, `HYGIENE profile-path <path>:<line>` once per file with the first match's line number (D1), `HYGIENE unreadable <path>` when the delegate throws (D3), then `HYGIENE Findings=<n>`. The script body after the functions is exactly: an `if ($MyInvocation.InvocationName -ne '.')` guard whose block assigns the result, writes `$result.Lines` to the pipeline and calls `exit $result.ExitCode`.
- Rules part file: `Get-UserProfilePathPattern` (returns the generic pattern string from spec.md, no parameters); `Find-UserProfilePathMatch -Text` (one record with a `LineNumber` property per matching line, case-insensitive, no text); `Get-RawEvidenceDocumentKind -RelativePath -Content` (extension gate first; for xml: strip a byte-order mark, skip the declaration, comments, processing instructions and a DOCTYPE, take the first element name terminated by whitespace, a greater-than sign or a slash; map per spec.md; a `report` root is `jacoco-raw` when the content matches the regular expression <(class|sourcefile|method|line)[\s>] written unbackticked here, else `jacoco-projection`).
- Git part file: `Invoke-GitExe -GitArgs [string[]]` (splats `& git @GitArgs 2>&1`, then calls `Assert-GitExitCode -ExitCode $LASTEXITCODE -GitArgs $GitArgs`, returns the captured output; the only place git is invoked); `Assert-GitExitCode -ExitCode -GitArgs` (pure; throws with the joined argument list in the message when ExitCode is non-zero; this is the function the wrapper-throw test exercises so no test invokes git); `Get-TrackedFileRecord` (calls `Invoke-GitExe -GitArgs @("ls-files", "--eol", "-z")`, joins the output, splits on NUL, parses each record into `Path` and `IsBinaryInIndex` where the latter is true when the attributes segment contains i/-text); `Read-TrackedFileText -Path -ReadContent [scriptblock] -IsBinaryInIndex [switch]` (the default delegate returns the file's bytes; a delegate result that is already a string is returned unchanged; a byte result is decoded by byte-order mark, UTF-8 with mark, UTF-16 little-endian, UTF-16 big-endian, otherwise UTF-8 without throwing; when IsBinaryInIndex is set and the bytes carry no UTF-16 mark the function returns `$null`, which the entry point treats as "not scanned for rule B" per D2). The default delegate returns the byte array wrapped with the unary comma operator, so PowerShell hands the array back as one object rather than unrolling it into the pipeline. A null or empty delegate result is empty text. The entry point skips rule B when the decoded text is empty. No parameter carries an Allow-prefixed validation attribute, because the P1-T7 and P6-T12 probes reject the substring allow; an optional delegate is therefore declared with a default value and tested for null in the body, not with an attribute. `Read-TrackedFileText` converts any non-null, non-string delegate result with `[byte[]]` before decoding, so a test delegate that returns a byte array without the unary comma (which PowerShell unrolls into an object array of bytes) is decoded the same way as the default delegate's wrapped array. `Get-RawEvidenceDocumentKind` declares `-Content` as optional with an empty-string default (a plain default value carrying no validation attribute, so the P1-T7 and P6-T12 probes for the substring allow stay at 0) and returns `none` for empty content only after the extension gate has run (a trx, coverage or coveragexml extension is classified by its extension whatever the content, per spec.md line 190; only an xml record with empty content returns `none`); the P2-T1 dot-sourced call therefore binds without error for a document whose `Get-Content -Raw` result is `$null`. The empty-string and null records in the P1-T3 zero-exit test use Markdown paths, so the extension gate classifies them `none` without reading content and rule B skips their empty text.
- Every new .ps1 file is UTF-8 with byte-order mark, ASCII content only, PowerShell 7 compatible, advanced functions with `[CmdletBinding()]`, singular nouns, no `Write-Host`, no `$env:` read, no `Get-Date`, no path or token allowlist, under 500 lines. Every violating fixture in the three test files is assembled by concatenation at run time (drive letter, colon, separator, the profile parent and a segment as separate literals); XML fixtures are here-strings; no test creates, writes or deletes a file.
- Tests dot-source the entry point (which dot-sources the two part files) from a `BeforeAll` block placed inside each `Describe` (never at file scope) using `$PSScriptRoot` and three `..` segments, so that while the entry point is absent the throw is attributed to that Describe and Pester reports every It in the block as Failed (the AC2 observation in P1-T4 depends on this placement); the tests mock `Invoke-GitExe` with the body `param([string[]]$GitArgs)` where a mock is needed, and inject content through `-ReadContent`.

### Phase 4 — Redaction sweep with the throwaway helper

Helper contract (binding on P4-T1; see D6, D17 and D18): the redaction helper at SCRATCH\Invoke-IdentifierRedaction.ps1 takes `-DryRun` (count only, write nothing), `-VerifyDiff` (read `git diff -U0 HEAD` for every path in its own log's written set, count removed and added content lines, count removed lines matching none of the eight patterns) and `-VerifyBom` (described below) and prints one labelled line per figure: `FILES-SCANNED=`, `FILES-SKIPPED-BINARY=`, `UTF16-SCANNED=`, `FILES-WITH-MATCHES=`, `FILES-WRITTEN=`, `FEATURE-FOLDER-WRITTEN=` (written paths under this feature folder), `RULE1=` to `RULE8=` (substitution counts; `RULE8=` is the D17 rule), `XML-REWRITTEN=`, `XML-REPARSE-FAILED=`, and in verify mode `REMOVED-LINES=`, `ADDED-LINES=`, `REMOVED-UNMATCHED=`, `VERIFY-DECODED-FILES=`, `VERIFY-WRITTEN-SET=`. The apply run saves the original bytes of every i/-text file it rewrites under SCRATCH (one copy per file under a SCRATCH sub-directory named originals, the copy named by the SHA-256 of the repository-relative path), because `git diff -U0 HEAD` prints no content lines for a path the index marks binary; `-VerifyDiff` decodes the saved copy and the working file by their byte-order marks, compares them line by line, adds the removed, added and unmatched figures from that comparison to the same three counts, and prints `VERIFY-DECODED-FILES=` (the number of saved copies compared). Its log SCRATCH\redaction.log carries one entry per written file (D6), so the log's entry set is the written set; no line of it and no saved copy is ever copied into the repository. A write-mode run whose `FILES-WRITTEN=` is 0 leaves SCRATCH\redaction.log and the SCRATCH originals sub-directory unchanged; a write-mode run with a non-empty written set replaces both. In verify mode the helper also prints `VERIFY-WRITTEN-SET=` (the number of paths in the written set it read from SCRATCH\redaction.log). The `-VerifyBom` switch reads the same log and, for every entry, compares the logged before class with the class it reads at verify time from the leading bytes of the working file (never the logged after class), and prints `BOM-COMPARED=` (the number of log entries whose working file was read and compared) and `BOM-MISMATCH=` (the number of those entries whose logged before class differs from the class read from the working file); P4-T6 reads both figures from that invocation, and because the P4-T5 second run writes nothing, the log both switches read is the one P4-T4 wrote. `-VerifyDiff` and `-VerifyBom` are read-only: neither performs a substitution and neither writes anything, including SCRATCH\redaction.log and the SCRATCH originals sub-directory, so a verify run leaves both exactly as the last write-mode run left them, whichever order P4-T6 and P4-T7 run in.

### Phase 6 — Final QA toolchain loops, footprint gates, guard confirmation and acceptance check-offs

Loop rule: P6-T1 to P6-T9 form one toolchain pass in this order (PowerShell format, analyze, Pester through the PoshQC test tool with the coverage figures read from CI in P6-T38 per Ruling 1; C# format, check, analyzer rebuild, nullable rebuild, MSTest with coverage). If any step fails or rewrites a tracked file, the executor fixes the cause inside the Write Set and restarts at P6-T1, with two exceptions: a P6-T5 formatter rewrite of a path listed under the P0-T11 `BASELINE-UNFORMATTED:`, restored with `git checkout -- <path>` and recorded under `RESTORED-BASELINE-UNFORMATTED:`, is not a rewrite for this rule, per P6-T5; and a P6-T6 non-zero exit recorded `BASELINE-RELATIVE: yes` (every reported path listed under the same iteration's P6-T5 `RESTORED-BASELINE-UNFORMATTED:`) is not a loop failure and does not restart the loop, per P6-T6 and P6-T10; every iteration is recorded with an `iterN` suffix inside the same artifact; P6-T10 records the clean pass. No step may be recorded as skipped. Loop commits: before restarting from an iteration N that rewrote or edited any tracked Write-Set path (a formatter rewrite, a restored byte-order mark, or a fix), the executor commits those paths with `git add -- <the rewritten paths>` followed by `git commit -m "style(927): apply formatter output from final QA iteration N" -m "Co-Authored-By: MODEL-NAME noreply@anthropic.com"` (N transcribed as the iteration number; these paths are non-exempt, so the P0-T3 checkpoint verified present is what admits the commit), records `git show --name-only --format= HEAD` under `LOOP-COMMIT-N:` in that iteration's `iterN` section, and only then restarts at P6-T1. This keeps every anchored diff and porcelain scope gate in P6-T13 and P6-T14 truthful, because a rewrite left uncommitted would otherwise sit in the working tree as an unexplained modification.

$fi = Get-Item -LiteralPath $p; "JUNIT-AGE-SECONDS=" + [int]((Get-Date).ToUniversalTime() - $fi.LastWriteTimeUtc).TotalSeconds; [xml]$x = Get-Content -LiteralPath $p -Raw; $root = $x.DocumentElement; "JUNIT-ROOT tests=" + $root.tests + " failures=" + $root.failures + " errors=" + $root.errors + " disabled=" + $root.disabled; $tc = @($x.SelectNodes("//testcase")); $passed = @($tc | Where-Object { $_.status -eq "Passed" }).Count; $failed = @($tc | Where-Object { $_.status -eq "Failed" -or $null -ne $_.SelectSingleNode("failure") -or $null -ne $_.SelectSingleNode("error") }).Count; $skipped = @($tc | Where-Object { $_.status -eq "Skipped" -or $null -ne $_.SelectSingleNode("skipped") }).Count; "PESTER Passed=" + $passed + " Failed=" + $failed + " Skipped=" + $skipped + " Total=" + $tc.Count; $hyg = @($tc | Where-Object { $_.classname -like "*tests?scripts?hygiene?*" }); "HYGIENE Passed=" + @($hyg | Where-Object { $_.status -eq "Passed" }).Count + " Failed=" + @($hyg | Where-Object { $_.status -ne "Passed" }).Count + " Skipped=" + @($hyg | Where-Object { $_.status -eq "Skipped" }).Count + " Total=" + $hyg.Count; foreach ($t in $hyg) { "IT| " + (Split-Path -Leaf $t.classname) + " | " + $t.name + " | " + $t.status }'` (the classname filter uses the single-character wildcard for the separator so it is channel- and platform-independent; the `PESTER` and `HYGIENE` lines are derived from the document, as the P3-T17 artifact derived its counts, and the artifact says so; the testcase `name` attribute is the Describe name, a dot, and the It name, as observed in the document the P3-T17 run left on disk, so the It names spec.md lists appear inside the third field of each `IT|` line).

  - Command: the pwsh payload fenced below (its filters name the governance directory, the two MCP configuration files and the sibling feature folders of the run, none of which this plan writes; the diff is anchored at the three-dot range per C7, revision 1.15, F1, round 12, and the only BASE-SHA token left in it is the operand of the second ancestry check, transcribed per C7); then `git status --porcelain`. Revision 1.15 (F2 Option B, round 12): the merge-base equality with the P0-T2 value is withdrawn, because the branch merges origin/main as the run directs and that merge necessarily moves the merge base; AC19's intent (no history rewrite, no force push, the branch still contains its own starting point) is carried by three ancestry checks, each expected to exit 0, plus a negative control that must exit 1 to show the check can fail. The payload fetches origin first so that the third check reads the pushed tip as it stands (a non-zero `FETCH-EXIT=` is recorded and the third check then reads the last fetched remote-tracking ref, which the artifact states). Control operand: the reversed operands of the first check (HEAD is not an ancestor of the P0-T2 merge base 177b6d78e, because HEAD carries every commit made since it), which exits 1 whenever HEAD has advanced past that merge base and needs no local ref. The ruled control ref prep/927-batched-plan-r1.10 is run as well and recorded, not gated: round 12 re-derived from the worktree reflog that its commit 1d7100762 is the parent of the plan-restore commit 94390f137 (a forward commit, not a reset), so it is an ancestor of HEAD and its `--is-ancestor` exit is expected to be 0; had it been the gating control, the control could never have read 1 and AC19 could never have been checked off.

```text
pwsh -NoProfile -Command '$plan = Get-Content -LiteralPath "docs/features/active/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927/plan.2026-09-28T19-44.md"; $start = [array]::IndexOf($plan, "## Write Set"); $end = $start + 1; while ($end -lt $plan.Count -and -not $plan[$end].StartsWith("## ")) { $end++ }; $bt = [string][char]96; $rx = $bt + "([^" + $bt + "]+)" + $bt; $tokens = @($plan[$start..($end - 1)] | ForEach-Object { [regex]::Matches($_, $rx) | ForEach-Object { $_.Groups[1].Value } }); $prefixes = @($tokens | Where-Object { $_.EndsWith("/**") } | ForEach-Object { $_.Substring(0, $_.Length - 2) }); $exact = @($tokens | Where-Object { -not $_.EndsWith("/**") }); git fetch origin 2>&1 | Out-Null; "FETCH-EXIT=" + $LASTEXITCODE; $mb = (git merge-base origin/main HEAD); "MERGE-BASE-NOW=" + $mb; git merge-base --is-ancestor 177b6d78e1b2408e5aedbd794cef3aad6b7fb372 $mb 2>&1 | Out-Null; "ANCESTRY-1-EXIT=" + $LASTEXITCODE; git merge-base --is-ancestor BASE-SHA HEAD 2>&1 | Out-Null; "ANCESTRY-2-EXIT=" + $LASTEXITCODE; git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/bug/evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927 HEAD 2>&1 | Out-Null; "ANCESTRY-3-EXIT=" + $LASTEXITCODE; git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD 177b6d78e1b2408e5aedbd794cef3aad6b7fb372 2>&1 | Out-Null; "ANCESTRY-CONTROL-EXIT=" + $LASTEXITCODE; git merge-base --is-ancestor prep/927-batched-plan-r1.10 HEAD 2>&1 | Out-Null; "ANCESTRY-PREP-REF-EXIT=" + $LASTEXITCODE; $d = @(git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD); "DIFF-PATHS=" + $d.Count; $out = @($d | Where-Object { $p = $_; -not (($exact -contains $p) -or (@($prefixes | Where-Object { $p.StartsWith($_) }).Count -gt 0)) }); "OUTSIDE-WRITE-SET=" + $out.Count; foreach ($o in $out) { "OUTSIDE| " + $o }; "GOVERNANCE=" + @($d | Where-Object { $_ -like ".claude/*" }).Count; "MCP-CONFIG=" + @($d | Where-Object { $_ -eq ".mcp.json" -or $_ -eq ".codex/config.toml" }).Count; "BUILD-INPUTS=" + @($d | Where-Object { $_ -match "\.(sln|csproj|props|targets)$" -or $_ -match "(^|/)(packages|app)\.config$" }).Count; "PROD-CS=" + @($d | Where-Object { $_ -match "\.cs$" -and $_ -notmatch "\.Test/" }).Count; "SIBLING-2026-09-28=" + @($d | Where-Object { $_ -like "docs/features/active/2026-09-28-*" -and $_ -notlike "docs/features/active/2026-09-28-evidence-and-identity-hygiene-sweep-927/*" }).Count'
```
